334229 Let $G$ be a simple graph of order $n$. A graph is **$3_t$-critical** if its total domination number is $3$ and adding an edge between any nonadjacent vertices lowers its total domination number. Suppose in addition that $G$ has diameter $2$, and let $m$ denote its number of edges. **Edge-size conjecture for diameter-2, $3_t$-critical graphs.** Then

$$
m\geq\frac{n(n-2)}{4}.
$$

A proof of this conjecture would, according to the source, imply the Murty–Simon conjecture together with the cited results for the diameter-3 case; the supplied text does not state that it has been resolved.
334231 Let $\operatorname{sProperad}$ be the category of simplicially enriched properads, and let

$$
N:\operatorname{sProperad}\rightarrow \operatorname{sSet}_{disc}^{\varGamma^{\operatorname{op}}}
$$

be the properadic nerve functor, where $\varGamma$ is the graphical category. **Properadic nerve Quillen-equivalence conjecture.** The properadic nerve functor $N$ is the right adjoint in a Quillen equivalence. This would relate the model theory of simplicially enriched properads to the conjectural model structure on discrete graphical spaces.
334243 Let $T$) be a locally finite tree, and let $G$ be a closed subgroup of ${\mathrm{Aut}}(T)$ acting transitively on the boundary $\partial T$.

**Type I conjecture.** Then $G$ is a type ${\rm I}$ group.

This conjecture asks for a characterization of type ${\rm I}$ closed subgroups of automorphism groups of trees. The paper studies this problem and proves broad non-type-I and non-amenability results, but the supplied text does not establish the conjecture in full.
334292 For $r\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{\infty\}$ and a graph $Q$, define

$$
\widetilde{\gamma}_r(Q)=\max_{H\subseteq Q}\gamma_r(H).
$$

Let $\chi(Q)$ denote the chromatic number of $Q$. **Edwards–King replacement conjecture.** If $Q$ is a line graph, then

$$
\chi(Q)\le\left\lceil\widetilde{\gamma}_\infty(Q)\right\rceil.
$$

This is proposed as a possible approach to the Edwards–King fractional chromatic conjecture; the supplied text gives no resolution.
334312 Let $mathcal G$ be a finite abelian group, let $D(mathcal G)$ denote its Davenport constant, and let $G$ be a graph. A graph is **zero-forcing for $mathcal G$** if every $mathcal G$-labeling of its vertices has a non-empty connected zero-sum subgraph. **Zero-forcing chromatic conjecture.** If

$$
\chi(G) \geq D(\mathcal G),
$$

then $G$ is zero-forcing for $mathcal G$. This would follow from Hadwiger's conjecture and would give a sufficient condition depending on the Davenport constant rather than the order of the group; it remains open.
334319 Let $G$ be a graph and let $z_0\in V(G)$. A graph is **$\langle\mathbb{Z}_3\rangle$-extendable at $z_0$** if every pre-orientation of the edges incident with $z_0$ whose out-degree minus in-degree at $z_0$ agrees modulo $3$ with a prescribed zero-sum function can be extended to the corresponding $\beta$-orientation of $G$. **Extendability conjecture.** Every $5$-edge-connected essentially $6$-edge-connected graph is $\langle\mathbb{Z}_3\rangle$-extendable at any vertex of degree $5$. This is presented as a strengthening of the Jaeger–Linial–Payan–Tarsi conjecture, and the paper states that it would imply that conjecture; its resolution is not supplied here.
334328 Let $\delta_n$ denote the staircase shape, let $\operatorname{RPP}(\delta_{n+2k}/\delta_n)$ be the reverse plane partitions of the skew shape $\delta_{n+2k}/\delta_n$, and let $E_k^*(q)$ be the relevant $q$-Eulerian polynomial. Define

$$
\widetilde{E}_k^*(q)=\frac{E_k^*(q)}{(1-q)\cdots(1-q^k)}
$$

and $N=k(k-1)(6n+8k-1)/6$. **Determinantal q-Eulerian conjecture.**

$$
\sum_{\pi\in\operatorname{RPP}(\delta_{n+2k}/\delta_n)}q^{|\pi|}=q^{-N}\det\left[\widetilde{E}_{2(n+i+j)-3}^*(q)\right]_{i,j=1}^k.
$$

This is proposed as an analogue of the preceding determinant formula for the corresponding generating function, and is supported only by preliminary computations in the supplied text.
334374 Let ${\mathcal{P}}_k^\text{irr}$ be the set of all irreducible polyhedral graphs with $k$ rigid vertices. **Finiteness conjecture.** Each set ${\mathcal{P}}_k^\text{irr}$ is finite. This conjecture asserts that, unlike reducible polyhedra, irreducible polyhedral graphs cannot have an unbounded number of non-rigid vertices while the number of rigid vertices remains fixed; the source indicates that this prediction is supported by further classifications but does not establish it.
334398 Let $G_2(1)$ be the indicated two-vertex graph, let $\sqcup$ denote disjoint union, and let a staircase graph mean a graph of the staircase type defined in the paper. **Staircase representation conjecture.** Every Cheeger graph other than

$$
G_2(1)\sqcup G_2(1)
$$

can be represented as a staircase graph. This daring conjecture would imply both the triangle-freeness and bipartiteness conjectures for Cheeger graphs. It is presented as an open problem.
334449 Let $G$ be a triangle-free $d$-regular graph and let $H$ be a loop-graph. Write $v(G)$ for the number of vertices of $G$ and $\hom(G,H)$ for the number of graph homomorphisms from $G$ to $H$. **Triangle-free homomorphism conjecture.**

$$
\hom(G,H)^{1/v(G)}\le \hom(K_{d,d},H)^{1/(2d)}.
$$

This would extend the known extremal inequality for bipartite regular graphs to all triangle-free regular graphs. The conjecture remains open.
334485 Let $q$ be a prime power and let $\lambda=(2n,2n-2,\ldots,4,2)$ be the 2-staircase partition. Let $P(s_\lambda\mapsto0)$ denote the probability that its Schur function evaluates to zero under the random specialization considered in the paper. **2-staircase probability conjecture.** For a 2-staircase $\lambda=(2n,2n-2,\cdots,4,2)$,

$$
P(s_\lambda\mapsto0)=\frac{q^2+q-1}{q^3}.
$$

The conjecture is based on numerical data for $n$ up to $7$ and concerns the generalized staircase family introduced in the paper; no general proof is given.
334556 Let $Q[\mathcal{X}_N]$ denote the quadrature rule associated with a spherical $t$-design consisting of $N$ points, and let $\mathrm{wce}(Q[\mathcal{X}_N])$ be its worst-case error.

**Worst-case error monotonicity conjecture.** For fixed $t$, if $N'>N$, then

$$
\mathrm{wce}(Q[\mathcal{X}_{N'}])\leq \mathrm{wce}(Q[\mathcal{X}_N]).
$$

The conjecture is motivated by numerical experiments comparing well-conditioned and efficient spherical $t$-designs. It predicts that increasing the number of points does not increase the worst-case error, but the source provides no proof or resolution.
334565 For integers $n\geqslant s\geqslant2$ with $n>ks$, let $C_n^{s-1}$ be the $(s-1)$-st power of the cycle of length $n$, and let $K(n,k,s)$ be the $s$-uniform hypergraph whose vertices are the independent $k$-sets of $C_n^{s-1}$, with an edge formed by every $s$ pairwise disjoint such sets. **Chromatic number conjecture.** For $n>ks$,

$$
\chi\bigl(K(n,k,s)\bigr)=\left\lceil\frac{n-ks+s}{s-1}\right\rceil.
$$

The paper attributes this conjecture to Alon, Dol'nikov and collaborators and notes that it is proved when $s$ is a power of $2$; the general case remains open.
334581 Let $D=\{1,j,k\}$, where $j$ is odd, $j\geqslant 3$, and $k$ is even. Here $\mu(D)$ denotes the supremum of the densities of sequences of non-negative integers whose pairwise differences do not lie in $D$.

**Carraher et al.'s odd-even density conjecture.** If $k\geqslant 3j$, then

$$
\mu(D)=\frac{k}{2(k+j)}.
$$

This conjecture was posed as part of a program determining the density of sequences avoiding prescribed separations. The supplied text does not state whether it has been proved or disproved.
334584 Let $T$ be a tournament. A **captain vertex** is a vertex with the captain property used in the paper, and let $A$ be the set of all captain vertices of $T$. **Captain inclusion conjecture.** Then

$$
A\subseteq \operatorname{TEQ}(T).
$$

The motivation is that a captain vertex together with any of its slaves dominates all other vertices, while a dominating singleton is the unique minimal TEQ-retentive set. It remains open whether every captain vertex belongs to the tournament equilibrium set.
334618 Let $H$ be a bipartite graph. Its **cut involution group** is the group of graph symmetries generated by cut involutions, and $H$ is **weakly norming** when its associated homomorphism-density functional defines a norm. **Weakly norming graph conjecture.** $H$ is weakly norming if it is edge-transitive under its cut involution group. The paper presents this as an open characterization problem; the condition is sufficient in the authors' examples, but its necessity and sufficiency were not established.
334619 Let $H$ be a bipartite graph, let $t_H(f)$ denote its homomorphism density functional, and let the **stable involution group** be the corresponding group of stable involutions of $H$. A graph is **norming** when this functional defines a norm. **Norming graph conjecture.** $H$ is norming if and only if it is edge-transitive under its stable involution group. The source describes this as a suspected necessary-and-sufficient characterization, analogous to the weakly norming conjectures, and gives only partial results toward it.
334626 Let $\mathcal{F}_n^\alpha(x)$ be the flag descent polynomial associated with the quotient $\mathbb{Z}_{\alpha}\wr \mathfrak{S}_n/H$. **Unimodality and palindromicity conjecture.** The polynomial $\mathcal{F}_n^\alpha(x)$ is unimodal and palindromic. This is motivated by computational evidence, alongside the paper's observation of a symmetric distribution of the flag descent statistic over the quotient; the conjectured unimodality remains unresolved in the supplied source.
334693 Let $G$ be a primitive basic permutation group. Say that $G$ satisfies condition (c) of Theorem 4 or condition (d) of Theorem 4 when it has the respective properties listed there.

**Road closure conjecture.** If $G$ does not satisfy condition (c) or (d) of Theorem 4, then $G$ has the road closure property.

The road closure property is equivalent to the $2$-id property for finite transitive permutation groups, and the surrounding results exclude transitive imprimitive and primitive non-basic groups. The claim concerns the remaining primitive basic cases, with computational tests reported elsewhere in the paper but no resolution stated here.
334834 Let $P_m \stackrel{\text{def}}{=} ([1,2],[1,3],\dots,[1,m])\in\Gamma_{n,m}$. For any $k$, define

$$
X(\{G\in\Gamma_{n,k}\mid P_m*G\in\mathop{\rm \mathfrak S}\nolimits_{n,k+m}^\varnothing\})
$$

and write its Laplacian as an element of the graph space:

$$
\Delta\bigl(X(\{G\in\Gamma_{n,k}\mid P_m*G\in\mathop{\rm \mathfrak S}\nolimits_{n,k+m}^\varnothing\})\bigr)=\sum_{H\in\Gamma_{n,k}}x_HH.
$$

**Sign conjecture.** All coefficients $x_H$ are integers of the same sign.

This conjecture is presented as a generalization of the paper's codimension-one theorem. The source provides explicit calculations motivating the claim, but the supplied text gives no resolution, so its status remains open.
